This was found only because `T9.8` is the first thing to have actually loaded one of these pages from a real, running `next start` in a real browser — `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` and `smoke:boot` (which only ever asks for `/` and `/api/health`, both outside this layout) all stay green through it, and `next dev` cannot start at all (`R-DEV`) to have caught it that way either. `NotificationBell` is `T9.7`'s, and nothing in that card's own verification rendered it inside `AppLayout` against a working server. Every screen in milestones 3, 4, 6, 7 and 9 depends on this layout; nothing beyond it could have been proven end to end until this was fixed. In-lane (`apps/web/app/(app)/layout.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/shell`), a genuine correctness defect blocking the very thing this card exists to prove, not a decision — CLAUDE.md §4/§7 calls this in scope to fix rather than merely report.
Nearest spec: CLAUDE.md code-structure rules (`apps/web/app/(app)/**` renders every authenticated screen); build plan T9.8, T9.7.

## 2026-09-03 — T4.0a — The gone-page marker is a Postgres enum column named `status`, values `live`/`gone`, matching `store_page_type`'s own shape
Decision: `store_pages` gets a new column `status`, typed with a new Postgres enum `store_page_status` (values `'live'`, `'gone'`), `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'live'`. No other column, no check-constrained text column.
Why: the founder decided the shape — a status field naming which condition the row is in, not a deleted-at date — but not the column name, the enum's exact values, or whether it is a native Postgres enum or a checked text column; the card leaves those three to this session and asks that they follow `store_page_type` (the existing enum on the same table, naming what kind of page a row is) unless there's a good reason not to. There is no such reason here: `store_page_type` is itself a closed, small, code-meaningful set of conditions on this exact table, which is precisely what `status` is too, so a second native enum keeps the table internally consistent rather than mixing an enum column with a checked-text one for no visible reason. `DEFAULT 'live'` on a `NOT NULL` column backfills every existing row without a table rewrite (Postgres computes it once at the DDL statement, not per row), which is what makes "every existing `store_pages` row reads as live" true the moment this migration applies, with nothing else run.
Only two values today, not three: the founder's brief names `moved` and `unreachable` as future possibilities, not present ones, and nothing in this card writes or reads any value — inventing a third value with no writer and no reader would be a guess at a shape nobody has specified yet. Adding it later is one line in a future migration (`ALTER TYPE store_page_status ADD VALUE 'moved'`), which is what "room for a further condition... later" means here — it does not mean this migration can be skipped when that day comes, only that it stays a small addition rather than a new column or a second schema wave.
Nearest spec: main §12.3 (content inventory); `DECISIONS.md` `2026-09-03 — FOUNDER — A deleted store page gets a status field`; `docs/overnight-state.md` founder question 1 and the `T4.0` preparation list, item 7.
## 2026-09-03 — T4.1 — `gate_decisions.outcome`'s actual vocabulary: nine strings, defined here as T4.0 asked

Decision: Gate 1 writes one of nine `outcome` strings (`packages/core/src/gates/gate1.ts`, `GATE1_OUTCOMES`): `admitted`, `admitted_pinned_despite_zero_volume`, `admitted_with_warning`, `converted_to_optimize`, `converted_to_refresh`, `held_insufficient_substance`, `rejected_off_catalog`, `rejected_zero_volume`, `rejected_not_winnable`.
Why: `gate_decisions.outcome` was left as free text specifically so each gate card could define its own vocabulary (DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.0). Splitting "converted" by which action it became (`_optimize` / `_refresh`) and splitting a demand-floor pass into three shapes (clean, pinned-past-zero, manual-warned-past-zero) is this card's own call, made because §8.5's audit posture ("log every gate decision … to audit drift") only works if the row says *which* of these five happened, not just that the topic was admitted — a pinned override and a manual warning are different events even though both end in a topic on the calendar.
Nearest spec: main §8.2 (the five checks), §8.6 (rejection cards), §8.7 (manual add's proceed/warn); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.0 (`outcome` left open for this card).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.1 — `gate_decisions.reason_user_facing` and `topics.why_line` hold a template key, never rendered prose

Decision: both columns are the reason card's `templateKey` (e.g. `gate1.rejected_zero_volume`) as plain text. The interpolation params, the retry condition, and the redirect URL — none of which either column has room for — live in `gate_decisions.scores_json` instead, as `{reasonCard, linkTask, substance, winnability, rulesVersion}`. `topics.why_line` carries the key alone; there is nowhere on `topics` to put params at all.
Why: invariant 8 ("every user-facing 'why' … renders from template strings … never from an LLM") is already implemented as a two-stage design elsewhere in this codebase — `packages/ui/src/opportunities/why.ts`'s `renderTemplatedLine` takes a `{templateKey, params}` pair and resolves it against `packages/ui/strings/*.json` at render time, and `opportunities.reason_template_key` / `reason_params_json` already store the un-rendered pair for exactly that reason ("there is deliberately no column holding rendered prose"). `gate_decisions` collapses that pair into one `text` column, which T4.0 left unexplained; reading the column as "the key, rendered downstream" rather than "the sentence, written here" is the only reading consistent with the rest of the codebase's own rule against backend-composed prose. This is a naming mismatch worth a founder's eyes — "user facing" reads as "the sentence" to anyone who has not read this entry — but not a reason to have Gate 1 (in `packages/core`, which cannot know the store's language) compose English.
Consequence flagged for whoever renders `topics.why_line` (T4.2, most likely): unlike `gate_decisions.reason_user_facing` (which has `scores_json` beside it for the params), a topic's own why-line has nowhere to carry interpolated numbers, so a topic-list rendering will show its static fallback sentence with no numbers filled in until `topics` gains a params column, in a later schema wave.
Nearest spec: main §9.6.8 / invariant 8 (template-only rendering); `packages/ui/src/opportunities/why.ts`, `packages/core/src/api/schemas.ts`'s `whyLineSchema` (the established `{templateKey, params}` shape).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.1 — Winnability always reads the Limited Intelligence constant; no GSC-calibrated model exists yet to read instead

Decision: `packages/jobs/src/generation/admit-manual-topic.ts`'s `winnabilityFor()` returns `gates.winnability.limited_intelligence_constant` for every account, whether or not Search Console is connected. `runGate1` itself takes `winnability` as a plain number, so a real calibration can replace this one function later with no change to the gate.
Why: main §9.6.4 describes real winnability as "difficulty discounted against the site's demonstrated reach — the position range the site actually achieves per GSC," but gives no formula, and nothing in the codebase computes a site's demonstrated reach from GSC data today (confirmed by search — the only two winnability numbers that exist anywhere are the constant and the floor, both already in `signals.config.yaml`). Building that calibration is Opportunity Engine scoring territory (main §7.6, §9.6.4; pointer map → Lane C's `packages/core/opportunities`), not Gate 1's job of comparing a number to a floor — and it is exactly the kind of formula invariant 9 says belongs in one place, not invented twice by two lanes that might disagree. Always using the conservative constant is never wrong in the unsafe direction (it can only under-admit, matching "Limited Intelligence only gets the easy wins"), so nothing about this choice can pass a topic that a real calibration would have refused.
Nearest spec: main §8.2 (winnability, "GSC calibration or a constant"), §9.6.4, §7.11 (Limited Intelligence's constant fallback).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.1 — A manually-added topic mints its own minimal `opportunities` row; converted candidates reuse an open one by (signal_type, entity_ref)

Decision: `packages/db/src/repositories/opportunities.ts`'s `insertMinimalOpportunity` writes `impact: 'low'`, `impactScore: 0`, `confidence: 0` on every row it creates — never a real score. A plain admitted/held/rejected manual candidate gets `signal_type: 'uncovered_commercial_query'`, `entity_type: 'query_cluster'`, `entity_ref` = the lower-cased cluster head, `status: 'scheduled'` (admitted) or `'blocked'` (held/rejected). A converted candidate gets `signal_type: 'cannibalization'`, `entity_type: 'url'`, `entity_ref` = the lower-cased matched URL, `status: 'new'` — and `findOpenOpportunity` is checked first, so a second manual attempt landing on the same page reuses the open row (invariant 10's dedupe) instead of fighting it for the partial unique index.
Why: `topics.opportunity_id` is `NOT NULL` with no hedge for manual topics (the column's own comment, `packages/db/src/schema/content-engine.ts`, T4.0), and this card's own briefing said as much — "a manually-added topic … and `topics.opportunity_id` as a plain FK are enough for this card." `signal_type: 'cannibalization'` for a conversion is not invented naming: main §8.2 calls the existing-target check "the cannibalization check" outright, so a row explaining why a CREATE became an OPTIMIZE is that signal by the spec's own word for it. Real impact/confidence scoring is explicitly "internal and versioned" (main §7.6) and Lane C's to build (T3.6/T3.7, currently blocked on a founder question — DECISIONS 2026-09-01/-02); zeroing both bands here is deliberate, not a placeholder guess at a number nobody asked for — an integrator or the founder should be able to tell these rows apart from real Opportunity Engine output at a glance, and a `0`/`low` pair that never occurs in real scoring is how. Flagged for the founder/integrator: once T3.6/T3.7 land, these placeholder rows and the real scoring formula will coexist in the same table, which is worth a look.
Nearest spec: main §7.6 (opportunity scoring, "internal and versioned"), §7.7 ("the cannibalization check"), §7.9 (dedupe); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.0 (`topics.opportunity_id`'s no-hedge NOT NULL).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.1 — Gate 1 checks the existing-target match before the substance floor, not after

Decision: in `runGate1`, the existing-target/cannibalization check runs immediately after the off-catalog check and before substance inventory — main §8.2 lists substance third and existing-target fourth, but that list is not an evaluation order.
Why: caught by this card's own integration test. A store with a strong existing match but nothing yet distilled for the candidate's family (because the family was only just formed, or genuinely thin) was coming back `held_insufficient_substance` instead of `converted_to_optimize` under the spec's listed order — telling a merchant to go add product details for an article that, because a page already covers the intent, was never going to be written regardless of how much substance the catalogue had. Checking existing-target first means a strong match short-circuits before substance is even asked about, which is also the cheaper order in the common case (existing-target's inputs are already fetched by the caller either way; substance requires a database read the caller only needs when a CREATE is genuinely still in play).
Nearest spec: main §7.7 step 3 ("do not CREATE… emit an OPTIMIZE… instead"), §8.2 (substance and existing-target, listed but not ordered).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.1 — Flagged, not decided: nothing turns a merchant's typed title into a query cluster (intent class + family mapping)

Not a decision — a gap surfaced because building around it silently would have meant guessing at product behaviour main §8.7 does not specify. `addTopicRequestSchema` (M0, `packages/core/src/api/schemas.ts`) collects only `{title, date, pin}` from the merchant; Gate 1 and `admitManualTopic` both need a full `QueryCluster` (search term, intent class, mapped family ids) to run at all, and nothing anywhere in the codebase turns free text into one. `admitManualTopic` therefore takes `cluster` as an explicit input rather than deriving it — whoever wires the real `POST /api/calendar/topics` route (T4.2, the route's owner) needs an answer to where that cluster comes from: matching the typed title against the account's own confirmed keywords/families (cheap, but a title that matches nothing yields an immediate off-catalog rejection with no research done), an LLM classification call (a cost and a latency Gate 1 is specifically supposed to avoid — main §8.2 calls the gate "~free"), or a UI change asking the merchant to also pick a family (which reaches into `packages/ui`, another lane's directory, and changes the already-frozen `addTopicRequestSchema`). This needs the founder's or integrator's call, not a guess made inside T4.2 under deadline.
Nearest spec: main §8.7 (manual add, "the user can add a topic by hand"); `packages/core/src/api/schemas.ts` `addTopicRequestSchema` (the frozen shape this collides with).
## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — Cannibalization resolves to FIX, not OPTIMIZE
Decision: `selectAction` maps every validated `cannibalization` signal to `FIX`. Main §7.8's own row allows either ("FIX / OPTIMIZE"), and the worked-example fixture (`action-selection.test.ts`) accepts both — this is the one this card commits to.
Why: every task the row itself names — primary-page designation, internal-link realignment, canonical recommendation, possible consolidation — is structural work on which page Google should treat as authoritative, not new copy on a page. `OPTIMIZE`'s own definition (main §7.4) is "unlock potential" on one page; cannibalization is never about one page's potential, it is about which of several already-adequate pages should keep the traffic. FIX also matches where the signal's tasks land in the `opportunity_tasks` enum: `primary_url` and `canonical_recommendation` have no OPTIMIZE-shaped equivalent.
Nearest spec: main §7.3 (cannibalization row), §7.8 (the mapping table), §11 ("duplicate/cannibalizing pages" is explicitly a FIX row).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — Two P0 signals have no detector yet; their shapes are declared, not built, so the engine can still score them
Decision: `existing_page_intent_gap` and `indexing_issue` — worked examples 4 and 8 — get thin, type-only signal shapes in `packages/core/src/opportunities/p1-signal-shapes.ts`, hand-fed with each worked example's own evidence in `action-selection.test.ts`, the same way `T3.5` hand-built worked example 3's input rather than editing the shared fixture (DECISIONS 2026-09-02 T3.5, and its scheduled audit's [LOW] finding on the same fixture).
Why: `existing_page_intent_gap`'s detection is main §10.3's subtopic-coverage analysis — an LLM call over a page and its top-5 SERP competitors — which belongs to Lane E's OPTIMIZE pipeline (`T6.2`), not this card's `packages/core/opportunities`. `indexing_issue` needs the URL Inspection API (main §12.4, P1, "not required for launch") and no card in the plan builds it yet. Both are still real V1 signals — main §7.3's own taxonomy, the `signal_type` enum, and this card's own done-when name them — so the opportunity engine has to be able to turn one into a scored, actioned opportunity even though nothing produces one today. Whichever card eventually builds the real detector should replace this file's export; nothing downstream (`action-selection.ts`, `tasks.ts`, `scoring.ts`) reads more than the fields named there.
Nearest spec: main §7.3 (the taxonomy), §7.8 (worked examples 4 and 8), §10.3 (intent-gap detection), §12.4 (URL Inspection, P1); build plan `T6.2`.

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — Scoring formulas main §9.6.4/§9.6.5 don't cover fall back to a traffic-magnitude proxy
Decision: `existingPageGain` in `build.ts` applies the exact §9.6.5 gain formula only to the three signals it names a target position for — `striking_distance`, `low_ctr_at_strong_rank`, `content_decay`. Every other non-CREATE signal (`cannibalization`, `missing_or_weak_metadata`, a competitor-gap OPTIMIZE, and the two P1-shaped signals above) falls back to `fallbackMagnitude`: the largest impressions-shaped number already in the signal's own evidence, or `1` when none exists. `product_family_coverage_gap` — which has no single keyword and so no `monthlySearchVolume` field at all — substitutes `keywordCandidatesClearingFloor` (the count of keyword candidates already clearing the demand floor) for `volume` in the CREATE formula.
Why: main §9.6.4/§9.6.5 give exact formulas for five signal shapes and are silent on the rest — this is a real gap in the spec's own coverage, not a threshold this card is free to invent from nothing. The alternative (leaving these signals unscored) would break `impact_score`/`impact`, which every opportunity's contract requires (main §7.6) and the Opportunities screen renders unconditionally. A monotonic, evidence-derived proxy keeps percentile ranking meaningful within each signal's own results without asserting a formula the spec never stated. Flagged rather than silently presented as the same formula the spec names.
Nearest spec: main §7.6, §9.6.4, §9.6.5 (the two stated formulas and their silence on the rest).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — Winnability and the CTR curve are inputs to this card, not outputs of it
Decision: `OpportunityBuildContext.winnability` and `.ctrCurve` are supplied by the caller. `createFamilyScore` spends `winnability` exactly as §9.6.4 states; nothing in this card fits a winnability model or a CTR curve from Search Console data.
Why: main §9.6.4 says the CREATE formula uses "the same inputs as Gate 1", and Gate 1's own calibration (main §8.2, "winnability with GSC calibration or constant") is explicitly Lane D's territory per the pointer map ("Gates 1–3, judge, calibration, override → main §8.2–8.6") and build plan §3 (`packages/core/{topics,calendar,gates,...}`, not `packages/core/opportunities`). Building a second, possibly-diverging winnability calculator in this card's own directory would risk two different numbers claiming to answer the same question main §9.6.4 says is one. `packages/core/src/signals/richness-gap.ts` (T3.4, already merged) already takes `winnability` as a caller-supplied field on its candidate type for the same reason — this card follows that precedent rather than introducing a second pattern. The CTR curve itself already has a real fitter in this lane (`packages/core/src/search/ctr-curve.ts`, T3.3); a caller assembling a build context passes its output straight through.
Nearest spec: main §8.2 (Gate 1's winnability), §9.6.4 ("same inputs as Gate 1"); build plan §3 (Lane D's directories).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — `opportunity_tasks.description` is composed as plain text in `packages/core`, not looked up from `packages/ui/strings`
Decision: `tasks.ts` writes each task's `description` as a template literal built from the signal's own evidence, directly in this card's own module.
Why: `CLAUDE.md`'s code-structure rule says copy lives in `packages/ui/strings/*.json` only — but that rule describes the why-line pattern (a template key + params, rendered at display time by `packages/ui`'s renderer), and `opportunity_tasks` was given a different shape by schema wave 2 (T2.0, already merged): `description: text NOT NULL`, with no companion template-key/params pair the way `opportunities.reason_template_key` has. Main §7.5 step 6 calls FIX/HOLD tasks "deterministic, templated from evidence" — the template is the code, and the schema stores the rendered result once rather than re-rendering it at display time. This card did not choose that shape; it is following the one already shipped. Flagged because it is a real tension with the stated code-structure rule, not because a card built it wrong — whoever next touches `opportunity_tasks` should know the column, not this card, is where the deviation originates.
Nearest spec: main §7.5 step 6; `packages/db/src/schema/opportunities.ts` (`opportunityTasks.description`, T2.0); `CLAUDE.md` code-structure rules ("Copy: `packages/ui/strings/*.json` only").

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — New `reason_template_key` values have no copy yet; that is Lane F's directory, not this card's
Decision: this card introduces new `reason_template_key` values (`striking_distance.optimize`, `content_decay.refresh`, `competitor_coverage_gap.create`, and others named in `reasons.ts`) but does not add sentences for them to `packages/ui/strings/en.json`. Two keys are deliberately *not* new: a competitor-gap OPTIMIZE and the existing-target check's own OPTIMIZE case both reuse the already-pinned `existing_target.prefer_optimize` alias (Appendix A's canonical sentence), and `catalog_richness_gap`'s reason reuses `quality_rejection.insufficient_richness` for the same reason — one sentence, not two near-copies of it (invariant 24).
Why: `packages/ui` is Lane F's directory (build plan §3), not this card's. The renderer (`packages/ui/src/opportunities/why.ts`, already built) already handles an unrecognised key gracefully — it shows `opportunities.whyUnavailable` rather than a raw key or nothing (see that file's own comment) — so this is a real, visible gap until copy is written, not a crash. Lane F's own mock fixtures (`packages/ui/src/opportunities/opportunities.test.ts`) already invented illustrative placeholder keys of their own (`striking_distance.page_one_intent_mismatch`, etc.) for UI development before this card existed; those do not match the keys this card actually produces, and reconciling the two is copy work for whoever owns `packages/ui/strings`, not a rename this card should make unilaterally on either side.
Nearest spec: main §7.1, §9.6.8 (template-only why-lines); invariant 8, invariant 24; build plan §3 (Lane F's directories).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — "≥2 independent sources agree" is read as ≥2 distinct evidence sources on the detection
Decision: `confidenceScore`'s `independent_sources_agree` point is earned when `new Set(evidence.map(f => f.source)).size >= independent_sources_min`.
Why: main §7.6 names the concept ("≥2 independent sources agree, e.g. GSC + SERP") without defining it at implementation level. Reading it off the evidence a detector already stamped with `source` per fact is the only reading available without a second, parallel bookkeeping structure — and it is exactly what the evidence's own `source` field exists to answer.
Nearest spec: main §7.6 (confidence points).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — Percentile rank uses the midpoint (tie-safe) convention, and the CREATE formula shifts by one to avoid `log(0)`
Decision: `percentileImpact` computes `((below + atOrBelow) / 2 / n) × 100` rather than a plain rank/n; `createOpportunityScore` uses `log(volume + 1)` rather than `log(volume)`.
Why: main §7.6 says only "percentile rank", one of several valid definitions — the midpoint convention is chosen because a multi-way tie (a real case: several candidates can share a raw score) would otherwise hand every tied candidate the same extreme percentile, silently inflating how many opportunities read as "high impact". `log(volume)` is undefined at `volume = 0`, reachable by a merchant-pinned, zero-volume topic that `gates.demand_floor.allow_zero_volume_when_pinned` explicitly allows through detection (main §7.10) — `log(volume + 1)` gives that candidate the lowest real score (zero) instead of `-Infinity`, and is unchanged for every candidate with real volume behind it.
Nearest spec: main §7.6 (impact percentile, the CREATE formula), §7.10 (the pinned-topic allowance the zero-volume case comes from).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — Opportunity status on creation: CREATE/REFRESH auto-accept, a non-empty precondition blocks, everything else starts `new`
Decision: `initialStatus` in `build.ts` returns `blocked` whenever `preconditions` is non-empty (regardless of action — this is what makes HOLD render as blocked, main §7.6), else `accepted` for CREATE/REFRESH, else `new`.
Why: main §7.9 states the autopilot policy in prose ("CREATE/REFRESH: auto-accept... OPTIMIZE: user-initiated... preconditions non-empty ⇒ status blocked/HOLD") without stating which check runs first when both could apply. Preconditions are checked first because a blocked CREATE that also read as `accepted` would contradict invariant text in the same section — an opportunity cannot be simultaneously "ready to auto-schedule" and "blocked on a precondition". `FIX` is treated like `OPTIMIZE` (starts `new`) rather than auto-accepted: main §7.9 makes FIX's automation conditional on `auto_repair` and mechanical-repair eligibility, neither of which this card's per-signal build function can determine without a database read the caller has not supplied, so the safer default (surfaced to the merchant, not silently executed) is taken.
Nearest spec: main §7.6, §7.9 (autopilot policy per action type).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — The status transition graph, and why `dismissed` only ever returns to `new`
Decision: `lifecycle.ts`'s `ALLOWED` graph is main §7.9's prose turned into edges: the happy path `new → accepted → scheduled → executing → completed`; any open status may move to `blocked` or `expired`; `blocked` returns to `new` or `accepted` once a precondition clears; `dismissed` returns only to `new` (never straight to `accepted`); `completed` and `expired` are terminal.
Why: main §7.9 states each edge individually but never assembles the full graph, so an omission (can `blocked` skip straight to `scheduled`? can `dismissed` return to `accepted`?) is this card's to decide rather than the spec's. `dismissed → new` only, not `dismissed → accepted`, is the one deliberate choice here: the "show dismissed, undo" feature (main §7.9) restores a signal to visibility, but a merchant who dismissed a CREATE and now wants it back should see it decided again — including a fresh chance to dismiss it once more — rather than have it silently re-enter the auto-accepted, auto-scheduled path it would have taken on first detection. A re-detected signal (the far more common path back from `dismissed`, since `dismissed_opportunities` prevents new detections from creating a competing row) never reaches this graph at all — it stays dismissed by construction.
Nearest spec: main §7.9 (lifecycle, dedupe, expiry & approval policy); invariant 15 (guarded transitions).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — `opportunities.entity_type`'s `url` and the `EntityRef` contract's `page` name the same thing; the mapper translates
Decision: `toContractOpportunity` (`packages/core/src/opportunities/contract-mapping.ts`) maps the database enum's `url` to the frozen `EntityRef.kind`'s `page` (and passes the other four values through unchanged). Neither shape is edited.
Why: found while wiring `DbOpportunitySource` — `opportunities.entity_type` (schema wave 2, T2.0, migration already applied) and `EntityRef['kind']` (the `T0.7` frozen contract, main §7.6's `entity_type + entity_ref`) were built by different cards at different times and disagree on the word for "a URL of the store's". Per the standing rule for exactly this situation (DECISIONS 2026-08-31 T0.7, "where the table and the contract differ, the table is authoritative and the producing card maps"), the table's word is kept and the contract-facing mapper translates it — the same shape of fix that entry already applied to `recommended_action`'s case and the missing confidence-band column.
Nearest spec: main §7.6 (`entity_type` + `entity_ref`); `packages/db/src/schema/enums.ts` (`opportunityEntityTypeEnum`); `packages/core/src/contracts/opportunities.ts` (`EntityRef`); DECISIONS 2026-08-31 T0.7.

## 2026-09-03 — T3.6 — `OpportunitySource`'s real implementation lives in `packages/jobs/scan`, mirroring `DbExistingTargetCheck`
Decision: `DbOpportunitySource` (`packages/jobs/src/scan/opportunity-source.ts`) implements the `OpportunitySource` seam by wrapping the new `acceptedContentOpportunities` repository read and `toContractOpportunity`. It is exported from `packages/jobs/src/scan`, not wired into any job or route yet — nothing in the plan consumes it before Lane D's `T4.2`/`T4.6` land, which have not started in this worktree.
Why: the seam's producer is listed as "C — T3.6/T3.7"; the repository read (a plain scoped query) is squarely this card's persistence work, and giving the contract a real implementation the moment its data source exists closes the seam early rather than leaving Lane D looking at a stub pool from `signals.fixtures` for another card cycle. It follows `T3.5`'s own precedent exactly: `DbExistingTargetCheck` in the same directory wraps a pure `packages/core` function with real reads and nothing else. Not wired into any job registration or route — that DI wiring belongs to whichever card actually starts consuming it (`T3.7`'s onboarding run, or Lane D's replenishment), since building the wiring without a real caller risks guessing at a shape the consumer will just have to redo.
Nearest spec: build plan §4 (seams table, `OpportunitySource` row); main §7.9 (auto-accepted CREATE/REFRESH).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — The T4.1 gap is resolved: one model call classifies a typed title, on the founder's authorisation

Decision: `POST /api/calendar/topics` (the manual-add route) turns the merchant's typed title into a `QueryCluster` with one LLM call — `packages/core/src/topics/classify.ts`'s `classifyManualTopic`, wired into the route by `packages/jobs/src/generation/add-manual-topic.ts`'s `addManualTopic`. Not built as a guess: the founder answered T4.1's flagged gap directly, relayed mid-card by a peer integrator session with the founder present, who had verified the codebase facts behind it first (`keywords` has no family or intent column; nothing anywhere derives an `IntentClass` from free text; `buildQueryClusters` needs GSC query rows with impressions, which free text is not). The founder's answer left two things to this card to decide and journal, which the next two entries cover.
Why: recorded here rather than only in code because this is the single biggest scope change mid-card — everything downstream (the prompt, the schema, the call type, the failure handling) exists because of this one relayed decision, not because this session inferred or guessed it.
Nearest spec: main §8.7 (manual add); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.1 (the flagged gap this resolves).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — The classify call's model, tier and prompt version — left to this card by the founder

Decision: a new `LlmCallType`, `topic_classify` (`packages/core/src/contracts/llm.ts`), tiered to `haiku` in `CALL_TYPE_TIER` (`packages/llm/src/models.ts`), with its own versioned prompt file `packages/llm/prompts/topic-classify.v1.md`.
Why: the founder's answer explicitly left "which model and prompt version" to this card. Haiku, not Sonnet, on the same reasoning `distill` and `preview` already use it: this is a structured classification over a short title and a list of the account's own family names — not open-ended reasoning, comparable in shape to distillation's fact extraction — and it sits on the request path of a merchant waiting for the "Add" button to resolve, where latency is felt directly (unlike `persona`/`seeds`, which run inside an async ingestion pipeline nobody is watching). `v1` because it is the first version, following the package's own "one file per prompt version, never edited in place" rule.
Nearest spec: main §14.2 (prompt versioning), §14.7 (`call_type` on every LLM event); `packages/llm/prompts/README.md`.

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — Classification failure degrades to pause, never guesses; an invented family id is dropped, not trusted

Decision: `classifyManualTopic` returns a typed failure (never throws past its own boundary) on a transport error, a validation failure after the model wrapper's own single repair attempt, or an empty head term. On that failure, `addManualTopic` writes nothing at all — no `topics` row, no `gate_decisions` row, because Gate 1 never ran and there is nothing yet for it to be about — and the route answers `outcome: 'rejected'` with `rejection: {templateKey: 'appendixA.outage', params: {}}`, main Appendix A's existing canonical outage copy ("Delayed — we paused this action rather than continue with lower-quality or stale data."), reused rather than a new template string invented for this one call site. Separately, `classifyManualTopic` filters the model's returned `familyIds` against the account's own family list and silently drops any id that is not on it, rather than trusting the model's own promise (stated in the prompt) never to invent one.
Why: the founder's answer explicitly left "what happens when the call fails, or returns a family id that doesn't exist" to this card, naming "degrade to pause rather than guess a family" (main §14.4's standing rule, applied everywhere else an external dependency can fail) as the shape to consider. Reusing `appendixA.outage` rather than inventing a new template key follows invariant 24 (canonical strings used verbatim) and the same reasoning `docs/audits/T0.5.md`-adjacent code already applies elsewhere: a second "something went wrong, try later" sentence is not a second idea. The family-id filter is the same posture applied one layer deeper — `topics.family_ids` has no foreign key to catch a bad id (DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.0's own note that array columns cannot carry one), so trusting the model's promise rather than checking it would let a hallucinated id sit in the calendar's data silently. Filtering rather than failing the whole classification over one bad id: a partially-good answer (some real family ids, one invented one) is still useful, and an empty result after filtering is not a special case — it flows into Gate 1's existing off-catalog rejection exactly the way a genuinely off-catalog topic already does.
Nearest spec: main §14.4 (degrade to pause), Appendix A (canonical outage copy); invariant 22, 24.

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — `TopicScheduler` places topics on the calendar; it does not run Gate 1

Decision: the real `TopicScheduler` (`packages/jobs/src/generation/topic-scheduler.ts`'s `DbTopicScheduler`) only decides *where* an already-accepted CREATE/REFRESH opportunity lands (the next open day, never displacing a pinned occupant) and writes the `topics` row. It does not call `runGate1`.
Why: the interface's own doc comment (`packages/core/src/contracts/opportunities.ts`) describes placement only — "picks the next open calendar day by default ... a pinned occupant is never displaced" — and says nothing about admission. Gate 1 needs inputs (measured search volume, the substance inventory, the existing-target check) this call is not given, and the build plan's own sequencing agrees: `T4.6`'s done-when names the flow "opportunity → topic → **gates** → article", listing gates as a step after the topic exists, not inside its scheduling. Running Gate 1 here would also mean guessing which caller (T3.7's onboarding seed, or T4.6's replenishment) is responsible for having already gated the opportunity, which is exactly the kind of cross-lane assumption CLAUDE.md says not to make silently.
Nearest spec: main §8.7 (calendar placement), §8.2 (Gate 1); build plan §4 (`TopicScheduler` contract row), T4.6's done-when.

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — Flagged: the `Opportunity` contract has no channel for a topic's intent class or family ids

Not fully a decision — a second, smaller version of the T4.1 gap, discovered while building `DbTopicScheduler` and worked around rather than silently guessed past. `topics.intent_class` is `NOT NULL` and `topics.family_ids` defaults to `'{}'`, but `Opportunity` (`packages/core/src/contracts/opportunities.ts`, frozen in M0) carries neither field, and `EvidenceFact.value` (`string | number`) has no way to carry an array at all — so `familyIds` cannot be reconstructed from an `Opportunity` under any reading of the current contract.
What `DbTopicScheduler` does about it: `intentClass` is read from an `intent_class` evidence fact — the one convention already established in this codebase (Gate 1's own `buildEvidence` writes exactly this key) — and the scheduler throws a `TopicSchedulingError` rather than guessing when that fact is absent or unrecognised, on the same degrade-to-pause reasoning as the two entries above. `familyIds` has no such fallback available at all and is written as `[]` — schema-legal, and not silently wrong (an article generated from a topic with no mapped families is a problem `T4.3`'s evidence-pack assembly will hit and can surface loudly, rather than one hidden here), but genuinely incomplete.
Why flagged rather than fixed: fixing it properly means either the `Opportunity` contract gaining fields (a frozen-seam change, "not changed without the integrator re-freezing it") or Lane C's still-unbuilt Opportunity Engine (`T3.6`/`T3.7`) adopting a documented evidence-fact convention for both fields — neither is this card's call to make alone. Whoever builds `T3.7`'s onboarding seed or `T4.6`'s replenishment scoring (the two real callers of `TopicScheduler.schedule`) will hit this the first time a real, non-fixture `Opportunity` reaches it, and needs the integrator's answer before then.
Nearest spec: main §8.1 ("a keyword opportunity mapped to one or more product families"), §7.6 (the opportunity object); `packages/core/src/contracts/opportunities.ts` (`Opportunity`, `EvidenceFact`).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — Reading the frozen `/api/calendar` conflict codes: one code covers three already-resolved states

Decision: veto's frozen conflict list (`packages/core/src/api/routes.ts`) names exactly one code, `topic_already_published`, for the whole route. `packages/core/src/calendar/transitions.ts`'s `isVetoable` allows veto from `planned`/`generating`/`in_review` (main §8.7's own text: free on `planned`, cancels publication on `generating`) and reuses `topic_already_published` for every other state a veto is attempted on — `published` itself, but also `rejected_by_gate` and the already-`vetoed` case — rather than inventing new codes the frozen `CONFLICT_CODES` enum does not have. Move's list names two: `topic_already_generating` for the one race the lock-semantics section is actually about (the daily job dequeued the topic under the merchant), and `topic_already_published` reused again as the catch-all for every other non-`planned` state (`in_review`, `rejected_by_gate`, `vetoed`) a move is attempted on.
Why: `CONFLICT_CODES` is a closed enum "adding one is a code change in both places, deliberately" (`packages/core/src/api/errors.ts`'s own comment), and `packages/core/src/api/routes.ts`/`errors.ts` sit in `packages/core/src/api`, which is not this card's directory (build plan §3: Lane D owns `packages/core/{topics,calendar,gates,generation,publish,repair}`). Reusing the nearest available code rather than adding a new one keeps this card inside its own lane; the messages the mismatched cases get (`CONFLICT_MESSAGES` in `apps/web/app/api/calendar/topics/_lib/mutations.ts`) are generic enough ("This topic has already resolved and can no longer be changed") to be honest about a `rejected_by_gate` or already-`vetoed` topic too, even though the code name says "published". This is a judgement call the frozen contract's own author did not have to make explicit, not a defect in the contract — flagged here so the integrator can see the reasoning rather than only the code.
Nearest spec: main §8.7 (veto/move mechanics), §14.3.1 (guarded transitions); `packages/core/src/api/routes.ts` (the frozen conflict lists), `errors.ts` (`CONFLICT_CODES`).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — What "the day is occupied" means for placement, move and add

Decision: a calendar date counts as occupied — refusing a new placement, or (for `add`) triggering `calendar_day_occupied` — exactly when it holds a `topics` row whose state is anything other than `vetoed`. A vetoed row's date is free again.
Why: main §8.7 says a veto "keeps the gap" ("replenishment fills it later") rather than deleting the row, and the schema has no unique constraint on `(account_id, scheduled_date)` for exactly this reason — a vetoed row and a later replacement can coexist on the same date as history. Everything else (`planned`, `generating`, `in_review`, `published`, `rejected_by_gate`) represents a day that already has a real plan, an active pipeline, or a resolved outcome sitting on it, and `packages/db/src/repositories/topics.ts`'s `occupiedDatesInRange`/`findTopicOnDate` both read it this way. Not dictated by any spec section directly; inferred from the "keeps the gap" sentence and the schema's own missing constraint, which together rule out any reading where a vetoed day stays blocked.
Nearest spec: main §8.7 ("the calendar keeps the gap by default"); `packages/db/src/schema/content-engine.ts` (`topics`, no unique index on the scheduled date).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — Calendar dates are plain UTC calendar days, matching the frontend's own convention

Decision: every "is this date in the past" check in this card's code (`earliestSchedulableDate`, `planMove`'s date guard, the add route's `parsed.data.date <= today`) compares plain `YYYY-MM-DD` strings against `toIsoDate(now)` — a UTC calendar day, not a value calibrated to the account's persona timezone.
Why: `packages/ui/src/content/calendar.ts` (built by Lane F, already merged) states the same rule outright — "A plain day, never an instant" — and its own `isoDay()`/`positionOf()` helpers are UTC-`toISOString().slice(0,10)`, exactly the arithmetic this card's `packages/core/src/calendar/placement.ts` duplicates independently (not shared code — `packages/ui` is Lane F's directory) rather than deriving from. Persona-timezone precision belongs to the publish-hour and daily-dequeue logic (main §9.1/§9.4, `T4.5`'s card), which decides *when today's one topic runs*; this card only decides *which day a chip sits on*, and the existing frontend contract already answers that question the same way.
Nearest spec: main §8.7; `packages/ui/src/content/calendar.ts` (`isoDay`, `positionOf`, `acceptsTopic` — the established convention this follows).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — Completing T4.1's unwired PostHog events; move and pin get none of their own

Decision: `admitManualTopic` now captures `gate_decision` (main §14.7's Quality event) alongside the `gate_decisions` row it already wrote, and `vetoTopic` captures `opportunity_status_changed` (`from`, `to: 'dismissed'`, `actor: 'user'`) when the veto's own opportunity-dismissal succeeds. Move and pin emit no PostHog event of their own.
Why: `gate_decision` and `opportunity_status_changed` are the two events main §14.7's taxonomy actually names that a calendar operation triggers — `gate_decision` was built into Gate 1's own module (`T4.1`) but only ever reached a structured log line, never a capture; wiring it now is this card's "calendar PostHog events" scope, since topic admission is fundamentally a calendar concern even though the gate itself is `T4.1`'s file. `opportunity_status_changed` fires only on veto because that is the one calendar operation that changes an *existing* opportunity's own state (main §7.9's `dismissed`) — move and pin change a `topics` row, not an `opportunities` row, and main §14.7's taxonomy is deliberately curated rather than "one event per verb"; inventing `topic_moved`/`topic_pinned` events nothing in the spec names would be exactly the kind of unrequested addition CLAUDE.md §4 asks to be proposed, not built silently. Not proposed here either, for lack of a clear use — nothing in main §16's metrics or tech §1's notification matrix reads for them.
Nearest spec: main §14.7 (event taxonomy); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.1 (`gate_decisions.reason_user_facing`, the entry this completes the wiring for).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — `GET /api/calendar`: no volume join, and the paused ribbon does not check Shopify's connection state

Decision: `topicSchema.monthlySearchVolume` is always `null` in this route's response. The `paused` field reflects the account's kill-switch state (`mayAccountWorkRun`) and lifecycle gate (`accountLifecycleGate` — billing, vacation mode, deletion) but never Shopify's own connection state.
Why: `topics` stores no volume of its own (main §13's summary lists none, and `T4.0`'s schema agrees), and the only way to answer it live is a per-topic join against `keywords` by `target_keyword` — a real query this route does not currently pay for a field no card's done-when reads; left `null` rather than built speculatively, per CLAUDE.md §4. The paused ribbon: ui spec §6.1 names three causes ("vacation mode / disconnected / kill-switch") behind one canonical copy, and this card's read-first list (main §8.7, §14.3.1; tech §3; ui §6.1 *interaction rules only*) does not cite main §14.4/§6.2, where Shopify's connection state (`shopify_conns.invalidated_at`) and its own paused-state reasoning live — that is Lane B's territory. `mayAccountWorkRun`/`accountLifecycleGate` are both pre-existing, reusable primitives (built for the daily-generation-cycle gate, `packages/jobs/src/runtime/gate.ts`) this card composes rather than owns; adding a third, Shopify-specific check would mean building new cross-lane logic this card was not asked to build. Recorded as a real, stated gap rather than swept quietly under "paused: false" — a merchant whose Shopify connection is the *only* thing wrong sees no ribbon from this route today.
Nearest spec: ui §6.1 ("Paused ribbons"); main §14.4 (degradation), §6.2 (Shopify connection) — both outside this card's read-first list.

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — Two new composition-root files, and a duplicated `seoProvider()` rather than a cross-lane import

Decision: `apps/web/app/api/calendar/_lib/config.ts` and `apps/web/app/api/calendar/topics/_lib/config.ts` are added to `eslint.config.mjs`'s existing composition-root exemption list (`sortiva/no-raw-db-access`), on the identical shape already recorded there for `apps/web/app/api/shopify/_lib/config.ts` and `apps/web/instrumentation.ts`: each hands a raw `db()` handle to a constructor (`AnthropicLlmClient`, `DataForSeoProvider`, `DbExistingTargetCheck`) or straight through as a deps field, and no query runs directly against it — every query a repository call later scopes with an `AccountScope`. Separately, the topics config duplicates the small `DataForSeoProvider`-or-`MockSeoDataProvider` selection `apps/web/app/api/shopify/_lib/config.ts`'s `seoProvider()` already implements, rather than importing that function from Lane B's directory.
Why: `eslint.config.mjs` is one of the build plan's named "ordered code" files — not lane-exclusive, but "integrator-resolved," and this session was told to say so in its report rather than assume the edit is invisible, which this entry and the final report both do. The duplication (rather than `import { seoProvider } from '../../shopify/_lib/config'`) is a judgement call in the other direction: `apps/web/app/api/shopify` is explicitly Lane B's owned directory (build plan §3), and importing a function from it — even read-only, even something as small as a fifteen-line singleton — creates a coupling between two lanes' route groups that the ownership table does not anticipate and that would make Lane B's file harder to change without checking whether Lane D's calendar routes still compile. `preview/_lib/config.ts` and `shopify/_lib/config.ts` already establish that duplicating a small process-wide singleton per route group, rather than sharing one, is this codebase's own convention for exactly this situation.
Nearest spec: build plan §3 (lane ownership), §3 "Files no lane owns" (ordered-code files); `apps/web/app/api/preview/_lib/config.ts`, `apps/web/app/api/shopify/_lib/config.ts` (the precedent).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.2 — A minimal `articles` repository, ahead of `T4.3`'s real one

Decision: `packages/db/src/repositories/articles.ts` is new — `insertArticleStub`, `findArticleByTopic`, `findArticlesByTopics`, `discardDraftForTopic` — and is deliberately the smallest possible slice: no metadata, no body, no delivery logic, just enough to let a topic's `generating`/`in_review` state have a real `draft`/`in_review` article row pointing at it, so `vetoTopic` has something real to discard and this card's own done-when ("veto of a `generating` topic parks the draft and never publishes") is proved against a real row rather than asserted from the topic's state alone.
Why: `articles` is a real table already in the schema (`T4.0`), with no repository yet because full article construction is `T4.3`–`T4.5`'s job, none of which is built. `packages/db/src/repositories` is explicitly not lane-exclusive (build plan §3), and this slice adds nothing `T4.3` will not still need to build around — it does not decide metadata shape, slug rules, or delivery, all of which stay `T4.3`'s to design. `insertArticleStub` exists only so this card's own tests can create a draft to discard; production code never calls it, because nothing in this card's scope generates a real draft.
Nearest spec: main §8.7 (lock semantics, "the draft is discarded"); build plan T4.2's done-when ("veto of a `generating` topic parks the draft and never publishes").

## 2026-09-03 — FOUNDER — A typed topic title becomes a QueryCluster through one model call on Add
Decision: when a merchant types an article idea into the calendar, a single model call turns that free text into the full `QueryCluster` the admission gate needs — search term, intent class, and family ids — at the moment they click Add. Not word-matching against the stored keyword list, and not a UI change collecting the family from the merchant.
Why: `T4.1` shipped `admitManualTopic` taking a `cluster` it cannot build, and nothing in the codebase can build one from text. Three facts were verified in the code before the founder was asked, because the cheap option sounded stronger than it is: `keywords` carries no family link and no intent column (read from the table definition, `packages/db/src/schema/keywords.ts`); nothing anywhere derives an `IntentClass` from text — every occurrence consumes or forwards one; and `buildQueryClusters` works from Search Console query rows with impressions, so it cannot take free text. The matching option would therefore have had to word-match family names *and* invent an intent classifier out of phrasing patterns, with a wrong family producing an article about the wrong product range and nothing on screen admitting a guess was made. The cost objection was also narrower than it appeared and the founder was told so: this gap exists only on the hand-typed path, because scan-produced topics already arrive with clusters built from Search Console data — so this runs once per manual add, not across a scan, and "Gate 1 must stay cheap" is about the bulk path. The existing demand-floor rule already answers the unrecognised-term case (rejected unless pinned).
Nearest spec: main §8.2 (Gate 1 admission, demand floor), §8.7 (the manual-add path); `packages/core/src/contracts/opportunities.ts` (`QueryCluster`); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.1.

## 2026-09-03 — R-STREAM — The change stream goes back on the stub report, and a test now enforces the rule its removal broke
Decision: `new doubles.StubCatalogEvents()` is restored in `scripts/stub-report.mjs`, and a new test (`packages/core/src/contracts/seams-wired.test.ts`) fails whenever a seam is absent from that report without its real implementation being constructed somewhere that is not a test. The consumer is **not** wired: `registerCatalogEventTasks` stays unregistered and `DatabaseCatalogEvents` unconstructed, which is the founder's call, taken on the record.
Why: the report exists to fail a milestone while the product is still running on a stand-in, and a seam leaves it by someone deleting a line — a hand judgement nothing checked. The change stream's line was removed with a note claiming it "is served in production by `DatabaseCatalogEvents`". That was false: the class is constructed nowhere outside its own test and the drain job is registered nowhere, so milestone 2's exit gate passed partly on a sentence that was not true. **What actually works is the writing half** — merchant changes really are recorded — and then nothing reads them. Restoring the line makes the report's own milestone form fail until something genuinely reads the stream, which is the behaviour that was lost. The test is the durable half: the defect was never really the deleted line, it was that a person's judgement was the only guard on it. Two seams currently sit off the report legitimately and are asserted as such rather than trusted — `DbNotificationEmitter` (built in the Shopify composition root) and `DbExistingTargetCheck` (built by the calendar's add-topic route, as of `T4.2`); a class constructing itself in its own file does not count, or a helper would satisfy the check without the product using it. Verified by deleting the restored line and confirming two tests go red. **Not wiring the reader** was chosen because it is a production behaviour change that would lie dormant anyway: the recurring schedule is off pending four handlers, so neither the change stream nor the nightly re-read runs on a clock today, and the drain lives in Lane C's directories, which another session held at the time.
Nearest spec: build plan §7 (`R-STREAM`); main §12.3, §14.1; the `T2.2` audit as recorded in `docs/overnight-state.md`.
## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — `articles` has nowhere to store a draft's body or metadata description; the pipeline stops at the in-memory `Draft`

Decision: `generateArticle` (`packages/jobs/src/generation/generate-article.ts`) produces a complete, checked `Draft` — title, meta description, intro, sections, FAQ, product mentions — and returns it in full, but writes only what `articles` (schema wave 3, `T4.0`) actually has a column for: `title`, `slug`, `target_keyword`, `state`. The generated body and meta description are not persisted anywhere. No migration is added — this card's own scope note is explicit that the tables it writes through "already exist from schema wave 3" and a feature card may not add one (build plan §3, CLAUDE.md's migration rule).
Why: reading the schema rather than assuming it — main §13's table summary and `packages/db/src/schema/content-engine.ts`'s `articles` definition both list only those four fields plus delivery/publish bookkeeping. Nothing about the claim-plan model, the section-shape model, or this card's own done-when items required a body column to exist to be satisfied: every done-when this card owns (Gate 2 rejecting a boilerplate pack, a buying-guide draft's axis-named sections, the grounding harness, no literal currency figure, the claim-plan-before-draft call order, the writer's prompt excluding the raw pack) is provable against the `Draft` object the pipeline already returns and persists to `article_claims`/`article_product_refs`, which do have columns. What is **not** provable, and does not exist as working software today: a generated draft surviving a process restart, being read back for review (`T4.5`'s `in_review` state), being graded by the judge (`T4.4`, which needs the body to grade), or being published (`T5.1`/`T5.2`, which need the body to render). This is the single most consequential gap this card found — every card downstream of `T4.3` in the M4/M5 plan needs somewhere to read a draft's body from, and none exists.
**Flagged for the founder/integrator, not decided here**: `articles` needs at least a body column (bytea, compressed, matching `store_pages.body_compressed`'s own precedent — the same reasoning: Postgres is the store, there is no blob service) and a meta-description column, added in a hot-add mini-wave or the next schema wave, before `T4.4` can be built for real. The shape of the body column itself is a real choice this card should not make blind: markdown text, structured JSON (sections/intro/FAQ, matching `Draft`'s own shape and avoiding a re-parse), or both (rendered HTML plus the structured source) — each has different consequences for how `T4.4`'s lints, `T5.1`'s export bundle, and a hypothetical future in-app preview would read it.
Nearest spec: main §13 (`articles`, `store_pages.body_compressed`); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.2 ("A minimal `articles` repository, ahead of `T4.3`'s real one" — the entry that first noted body/metadata shape was left to this card, without noting that no column exists to hold either).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — Product images stay empty: `products` has no column to hold a Shopify image URL, confirmed for the evidence pack and the draft

Decision: `EvidencePackProduct.images` (`packages/core/src/generation/evidence-pack.ts`) and the jobs-layer assembly that fills it (`packages/jobs/src/generation/assemble-evidence-pack.ts`) are written against the shape a catalog image would carry once one exists, but always populate an empty array today — `packages/core/src/catalog/products.ts`'s own `productContentChecksum` reads `product.images` off the Shopify payload (to detect a change) and then drops it; nothing downstream of that ever stores an image URL. `catalogImagesFor` (`packages/core/src/generation/images.ts`) is therefore a correct, tested function that produces nothing for every account today.
Why: this is not a new discovery — DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T2.7 already found and flagged the identical gap for `top_products.imageUrl` ("the catalogue sync never persisted one"). Recorded again here, tied to this card specifically, because a reader of `T4.3` who sees "images from catalog only" in main §9.2 and an empty result needs to know this is the same unresolved gap and not a bug this card introduced — and because this card's own done-when ("images are catalog URLs only... no generated/proxied image") is satisfied by the mechanism being correct, not by there being any image to show yet.
Nearest spec: main §9.2 ("product images from the catalog only"); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T2.7 (the same gap, first found).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — Gate 2 lives in `packages/core/src/generation`, not `packages/core/src/gates`

Decision: `runGate2` (`packages/core/src/generation/gate2.ts`) sits beside the evidence-pack and claim-plan code it reads, in the `generation` directory the build plan names for this card, rather than in `packages/core/src/gates`, where Gate 1 (`T4.1`) lives.
Why: the build plan's own lane-D directory list names both `gates` and `generation` as separate directories this lane owns, and Gate 2 is defined by main §8.3 as "after research, before drafting" — structurally part of article construction, reading the pack `generation`'s own assembly code produces, rather than the topic-admission concern `gates` holds today. `gates` stays Gate 1's home; `T4.4`'s Gate 3 is free to pick either, on its own reasoning, when it lands — this is not a claim that all three gates must share one directory.
Nearest spec: build plan §3 (lane D's directories: `topics, calendar, gates, generation, publish, repair`); main §8.2/§8.3 (Gate 1 vs Gate 2's place in the pipeline).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — The claim plan splits deterministic claims (code) from judged claims (one model call), not four kinds all decided by the model

Decision: `packages/core/src/generation/claims.ts` derives `merchant_fact` claims (one per populated fact-sheet field) and `derived_fact` claims (one arithmetic comparison per numeric field per family, bounded to the widest spread) with no model call at all — pure functions over the evidence pack. `packages/core/src/generation/claim-plan.ts`'s `claim_plan` model call is asked for the other two kinds only: `recommendation` (a judgement, must cite claim ids it rests on) and `external_fact` (must quote a given competitor excerpt verbatim). The response schema's `kind` enum literally excludes `merchant_fact`/`derived_fact`, so the model cannot mislabel one even by mistake.
Why: `docs/content-pointers.md` §1 names four claim kinds and says "derived facts get re-derived in code... if the source numbers disagree, that is caught by a comparison, not by a judge" — which only makes sense if the derivation itself happens in code, not as a model's arithmetic. Extending the same reasoning to merchant facts: a merchant fact is by definition "the store's own data only," already structured in `product_facts.facts_json`, so asking a model to restate it is a place a transcription error could enter that a direct read cannot. Recommendations and external facts are the two kinds `content-pointers.md` itself describes in terms that require judgement (a value assessment; deciding which competitor passage is worth quoting) — those, and only those, go to the model, and even then their output is verified in code before acceptance (an external fact's quote must appear verbatim in the given excerpt; a recommendation's `evidenceRefs` must resolve to a real claim), never trusted outright.
Nearest spec: `docs/content-pointers.md` §1 (the four kinds, and "derived facts get re-derived in code").

## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — Citation and product-reference token syntax: `[[cN]]` and `{{pN}}`, chosen because they are unambiguous and cheap to strip

Decision: the writer cites an approved claim inline as `[[c3]]` and marks a product mention as `{{p1}}`, declared in a parallel `productMentions` array with which fields it renders. Both are plain regex-extractable tokens (`packages/core/src/generation/checks.ts`'s `CLAIM_MARKER`, `packages/core/src/generation/product-refs.ts`'s `PLACEHOLDER`).
Why: `docs/content-pointers.md` §2 gives the citation marker's example verbatim (`The tank holds 20 litres[[c3]]`) but not the product-reference token's syntax, and DECISIONS 2026-09-01's founder decision only says the body "carries a placeholder" without naming its form. Double-brace was chosen over double-bracket for the product token specifically so the two are never visually or programmatically confusable — a citation marker and a value placeholder are different mechanisms serving different downstream consumers (the grounding check vs. the publish-time resolver) and conflating their syntax risks conflating their handling.
Nearest spec: `docs/content-pointers.md` §2 (citation marker, given); founder decision 2026-09-01 (`DECISIONS.md`, "the body stores a reference... placeholder" — form not specified).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — The writer's prompt names citable products by id and title only, never their fact sheet or description

Decision: `CitableProduct` (`packages/core/src/generation/draft.ts`) carries exactly `{id, productId, title}`. The writer's prompt lists these so a "Products" section or a recommendation can name a real product by id; it carries nothing else about that product.
Why: `docs/content-pointers.md` §1's guarantee is "the writer only ever sees the approved claims, never the raw evidence pack" — read strictly, a product's fact sheet is squarely inside "the raw evidence pack" and must not reach the writer through a second channel that isn't called "claims." A title is not a fact about the product a claim could assert or fail to cover; it is the product's name, needed only so the writer can correctly attach a `{{id}}` token to the right item in a list. Tested directly (`generate-article.test.ts`'s "the writer's own prompt carried the claim list... not the raw competitor excerpt" assertion, and `draft.test.ts`'s "never carries a fact that is not in the approved claim list").
Nearest spec: `docs/content-pointers.md` §1 ("the writer... expresses an approved list rather than producing prose that gets policed afterwards").

## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — `topics.rejectTopicByGateGuarded` writes no reason; the calendar already has one in `gate_decisions`

Decision: `rejectTopicByGateGuarded` (`packages/db/src/repositories/topics.ts`) transitions `generating → rejected_by_gate` and touches no other column — specifically not `topics.veto_reason`.
Why: `topics.veto_reason` is the merchant's own veto text (main §8.7); a gate's rejection reason is a different thing that already has a home — `T4.2`'s `latestGateDecisionsForTopics` reads `gate_decisions.reason_user_facing`, keyed by `(topic_id, gate)`, for exactly this purpose ("`GET /api/calendar`'s `rejection` field reads this for a `rejected_by_gate` day"). Writing the same words into `veto_reason` too would give the calendar two places to look for one sentence, and the two could drift.
Nearest spec: main §8.7 (calendar transparency); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.2 (`gate_decisions.reason_user_facing`, the entry this reuses rather than duplicates).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — Section shapes: seven, mapped from the four `topics.intent_class` values by a keyword heuristic, not a new column

Decision: `packages/core/src/generation/shapes.ts` defines all seven shapes `docs/content-pointers.md` §4 names (the two the product design already had — `buying_guide`, `comparison` — plus the five with a newly-named failure condition: `sizing`, `how_to`, `troubleshooting`, `informational_commercial`, `category_explainer`), each with its own section skeleton and failure condition. `selectShape` maps the four persisted `topics.intent_class` values onto one of the seven, deterministically, with no model call: `buying_guide`→`buying_guide`, `comparison`→`comparison` one-to-one; `how_to`→`troubleshooting` when the cluster's own words look like a problem ("broken", "not working", "fix", …), else `how_to`; `informational`→`sizing` when the words look like a size query, else `category_explainer`. `informational_commercial` is implemented (skeleton, failure condition, and `checkShape` all exist and are tested) but the heuristic never selects it — nothing in the data available at this point in the pipeline distinguishes a commercial-decision informational query from a plain category one.
Why: `topics.intent_class` is a closed four-value Postgres enum from schema wave 3 (`T4.0`), and this card may not add a migration — so a seven-value "shape" cannot become a stored column without a schema-wave request this card is not making. The mapping is real content substance, not free: which shape an article gets governs its structure and its failure condition, which is exactly the kind of choice CLAUDE.md §3 asks to be surfaced rather than guessed past silently. It is judged safe to decide within this card and journal rather than stop and ask, on two grounds: it is entirely internal to Lane D's own `generation` module (no other lane's interface reads `ArticleShape`, and `topics.intent_class` itself is untouched), and it is cheaply reversible — a later card can replace `selectShape`'s heuristic with a real classifier, or extend `QueryCluster` with an explicit shape field, without touching any shape's skeleton, failure condition, or the callers of `sectionsFor`/`checkShape`. The `informational_commercial` gap is left open rather than papered over with a guessed keyword list, because a wrong guess here (treating a plain "what is X" query as "should I buy X") would misshape a real article, and main §4's own table gives no rule to tell the two apart from a keyword alone.
Nearest spec: `docs/content-pointers.md` §4 (the seven shapes, the "already picks a shape" baseline, the failure-condition table); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.0 (`topics.intent_class`'s four-value enum, closed).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — `evidence_pack_check` and `generation` join `packages/rules`, another lane's package, following the standing precedent

Decision: `packages/rules/signals.config.yaml` gains `gates.evidence_pack_check` (Gate 2's distinct-claim floor and boilerplate ratio) and a new top-level `generation` block (`length`, `internal_links`), both `UNSIGNED` — no founder-calibrated numbers exist for either, so the starting values are stated heuristics with a plain-language note on what each decides, per invariant 9's own requirement. `packages/rules/src/types.ts` and `schema/signals.config.schema.json` are extended to match; `rules_version` changes as a result.
Why: invariant 9 forbids any threshold literal outside `packages/rules`, and Gate 2's floor and the length-matching band are both exactly the kind of number that rule is about — they decide whether a pack is judged rich enough to write from and how long the resulting draft is asked to be. `packages/rules` belongs to Lane C after M0 (build plan §3), but the precedent for another lane adding a block to it — rather than inventing a second, unversioned threshold file — is already established repeatedly (DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T2.6, T3.4; DECISIONS 2026-09-02 T2.3's read-only reuse of the same file). **Flagged for the integrator, as those entries also flag**: this changes `rules_version` for every lane, which is `rules_version`'s intended behaviour for any config change, but is worth knowing at merge time. One block was added to `gates`, one new top-level block added; nothing existing was edited.
Nearest spec: main §7.10, invariant 9 (thresholds live in `packages/rules`); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T2.6 ("another lane's package" — the precedent this follows).

## 2026-09-03 — T4.3 — Gate 2's boilerplate check groups by `(family, field, value)`, not by raw text similarity

Decision: `runGate2` (`packages/core/src/generation/gate2.ts`) calls a fact-sheet value boilerplate when it is stated identically (case-insensitive, trimmed) by at least `boilerplate_repeat_share_min` of a family's contributing products, and holds the pack when the boilerplate share of all candidate claims meets or exceeds `boilerplate_ratio_max`.
Why: main §8.3 names the failure ("a 90%-boilerplate pack is killed here") without defining what "boilerplate" means mechanically. Reusing the exact identity `signals/substance.ts`'s `distinctFactsOf` already uses (`field=value`, scoped per family here rather than per account) keeps one definition of "the same fact" across the substance floor (Gate 1) and this check, rather than inventing a second similarity measure — embeddings or n-gram overlap — that could disagree with the first about whether two products "say the same thing." The cost: this only catches a fact repeated *verbatim*; two products describing the same material in different words are not caught, and are Gate 3's near-duplicate-prose lint's job instead (`T4.4`), not this cheap pre-check's.
Nearest spec: main §8.3 ("a 90%-boilerplate pack is killed here"); `packages/core/src/signals/substance.ts` (`distinctFactsOf`, the identity reused).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.7 — The HIGH audit finding is closed: re-detection now reconciles an open row's status against its own fresh preconditions

Decision: `reconcileStatusWithPreconditions(current, action, preconditionsNowEmpty)` (`packages/core/src/opportunities/lifecycle.ts`) is a new pure function the weekly/onboarding/event scan calls after every re-detection. It moves `new`/`accepted` → `blocked` the instant this pass's freshly-computed preconditions are non-empty, and moves `blocked` → `accepted` (CREATE/REFRESH) or `new` (everything else) the instant they clear — using edges `T3.6`'s own lifecycle graph already allows, never a new one. `scheduled`/`executing`/`completed`/`dismissed`/`expired` rows are never touched by this function; it answers `null` for all of them.
Why: `T3.6`'s scheduled audit (2026-09-03, `docs/overnight-state.md`) traced a real, reachable gap and named it explicitly for this card to address rather than let slip through untested: `upsertOpportunity` deliberately never writes `status` on a re-detection (protecting a merchant's in-progress work from being reset backward — the one direction `T3.6`'s own journal defended), but the same silence means the *opposite*, unsafe direction goes unaddressed too — a technical blocker discovered after a CREATE/REFRESH auto-accepted never actually re-blocks it, and `acceptedContentOpportunities()` (the exact seam Lane D's replenishment/calendar-seeding reads) keeps offering a row that should no longer be offered. Main §7.9 also promises the opposite honestly: a `blocked` row is "re-evaluated at the next scan after the underlying data changes," and the HOLD view's own copy (ui §5.3) says "we'll re-check automatically." Closing only the unsafe half and leaving the promise unbuilt would have been inconsistent with the spec's own text, so both directions are built together, symmetrically, from the same fresh preconditions this pass already computes for every candidate (recomputing preconditions is inherent to how detection works — a re-scan calls `selectAction` again regardless). Deliberately scoped to `new`/`accepted`/`blocked` only: once a row is `scheduled` or `executing`, it has a `topics` row and Lane D's calendar state machine (`T4.2`) is what owns further changes to it — reaching past that boundary from a signal-detection pass would be exactly the cross-lane reach `CLAUDE.md` §5 asks a lane not to make. The unsafe half is the one that actually matters for the HIGH finding (it fires strictly before Lane D ever reads the row); the safe half (unblocking) is a smaller, lower-stakes addition made because the spec's own copy promises it and the mechanism to build it correctly was already in hand.
Nearest spec: main §7.9 (lifecycle, "re-evaluated automatically"); ui §5.3 (HOLD view's own copy); `T3.6`'s scheduled audit HIGH finding (`docs/overnight-state.md`, "Audit findings, unactioned" → `T3.6`); invariant 15 (guarded transitions — the actual DB write this drives is `transitionOpportunityStatus`, already guarded).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.7 — A deterministic heuristic stands in for keyword intent/family classification, because nothing in the codebase can answer it today, and neither an LLM call nor a schema fix is reachable from this card

Decision: `classifyKeywordIntent`/`mapKeywordToFamilies` (`packages/core/src/signals/keyword-classify.ts`) are new, free, pattern-matching functions — intent from a small ordered set of phrase patterns (comparison / buying-guide / how-to / else informational), family mapping from significant-word overlap between the keyword and a family's name + differentiation axes (the same word-containment honesty `T3.5` already chose for query clustering, DECISIONS 2026-09-02 T3.5). Both are computed fresh at scan time, in memory, never persisted. Four of the nine P0 detectors this card must run — `uncovered_commercial_query`, `competitor_coverage_gap`, `product_family_coverage_gap`, `catalog_richness_gap` — take a `KeywordCandidate` (or, for the family-coverage-gap case, a caller-supplied `IntentClass`) carrying `intentClass`/`familyIds`, and this scan's own assembly layer (`packages/jobs/src/scan/assemble.ts`) is where these two functions actually get called.
Why: checked directly, not assumed — `keywords` (schema wave 2, `T2.6`) has no intent or family column at all (`packages/db/src/schema/keywords.ts`), and nothing anywhere in the codebase derives an `IntentClass` from free text; `T4.2`'s own decision journal (2026-09-03) already names exactly this gap for the sibling manual-add problem and records the founder's own verification of the same two facts. Main §6.6 says a seed keyword is generated *from* a specific product family's axes at enrichment time, so the lineage exists the moment DataForSEO enriches it — and there is simply nowhere for that lineage to be written, because the column does not exist and this card may not migrate one. The founder's answer to the sibling gap (`T4.2`) was a real, billable LLM call (`topic-classify.v1`) — deliberately not available here: main §7.5 step 1 states signal detection runs with "no LLM, no billable calls," a rule this card may not set aside for a step that runs over every confirmed keyword on every scan (unlike the manual-add path's one merchant action at a time). With the accurate route forbidden by spec and the correct route (persisting the lineage at enrichment, a `T2.6`/schema-wave fix) unreachable from this card's directories, the remaining option is a heuristic — free, deterministic, and honestly worse than what the store's own generation process already knew and threw away. **Flagged prominently, not shipped quietly**: recommend a founder/integrator decision to persist the origin family and a real intent classification at keyword enrichment time (a billable step already, unlike detection) in a later schema wave — that fix would strictly dominate this heuristic and this heuristic should be replaced the day it lands, not layered under it.
Nearest spec: main §6.6 (seed keywords generated from family axes), §7.5 step 1 ("no LLM, no billable calls" at detection); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.2 (the sibling gap, its founder answer, and the two facts it already verified); DECISIONS 2026-09-02 T3.5 (word-containment precedent).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.7 — `intent_class`/`family_id` evidence facts, completed for `competitor_coverage_gap` and `product_family_coverage_gap`

Decision: both signals now stamp an `intent_class` fact and one `family_id` fact per mapped family (repeated key — `EvidenceFact.value` cannot hold an array, so a repeated key is the encoding), matching `uncovered_commercial_query`'s own convention (already correct, T3.6) and the one `DbTopicScheduler` already reads (DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.2). `competitor_coverage_gap`'s data was already present on the candidate (`KeywordCandidate.intentClass`/`familyIds`) and simply never stamped; `product_family_coverage_gap` gained a new required `intentClass` field on its candidate type (see the heuristic entry above for where the caller gets the value).
Why: `T4.2`'s own decision journal names this exact gap and traces it to the first real caller of `TopicScheduler.schedule()` with a non-fixture `Opportunity` — this card is that caller. Without it, every accepted `competitor_coverage_gap`/`product_family_coverage_gap` CREATE opportunity this scan produces would make `DbTopicScheduler` throw `TopicSchedulingError` the moment the onboarding run tries to seed the calendar with it, which would make this card's own done-when ("full run on the synthetic store produces the expected opportunity set... in both full and limited modes") fail outright for two of the three CREATE-producing signals. This is squarely reachable from Lane C's own directories (`packages/core/src/signals`) and touches no frozen contract — the `Opportunity`/`EvidenceFact` shapes are unchanged; only the *contents* of an evidence array a detector already produces gained two more facts.
Nearest spec: main §7.6 (`evidence` — auditable, source-stamped facts); DECISIONS 2026-09-03 T4.2 (the flagged gap this closes the reachable half of).

## 2026-09-03 — T3.7 — Expiry, like the status reconciliation above, is scoped to `new`/`accepted`/`blocked`

Decision: the scan's own expiry sweep (main §7.9: "expires those whose evidence no longer holds") only expires an open row whose signal type this pass evaluated and whose entity this pass did not re-detect at all — and, like `reconcileStatusWithPreconditions`, only for rows still in `new`, `accepted` or `blocked`. A `scheduled`/`executing` row (one with a `topics` row already pointing at it) is never touched by this sweep, however stale its evidence.
Why: the same boundary as the HIGH-finding fix, for the same reason — once a row is `scheduled`/`executing` it belongs to Lane D's calendar state machine (`T4.2`), and this card's directories stop at `packages/core/opportunities`/`packages/jobs/scan`.
